This suggests a powerful construction: use profiles from the central binomial sequence, possibly reversed, to make convolution coefficients constant over intervals. The exact identity τ*τ=1 for first L coefficients can be used to create flat regions. By concatenating blocks with masses and signs (but nonnegative) and using τ or reversed τ, we can balance coefficients. This is like using a "basis" of flat convolutions.

Let's define `t_k = binom(2k,k)/4^k` for k=0..L-1. It satisfies ∑_{i=0}^k t_i t_{k-i}=1 for 0≤k≤L-1 (first L coefficients). Also for k beyond L-1, coefficients vary (increase? Actually for infinite t* t =1 all k; truncation causes after L coefficients = coefficients missing tail, maybe less). The tail t has infinite norm? finite.

If we set a_i = c * t_i for first L (mass c), its convolution has first L coefficients c^2 (flat). The later coefficients are c^2 * q_k where q_k = tail correction, maybe less than1? Let's compute q_k for k≥L: ∑_{i=0}^{L-1} t_i t_{k-i}. Since infinite sum=1, q_k =1 - ∑_{i=L}^{∞} t_i t_{k-i}. So <1. Thus self contribution decreases after L. Good. Thus a one-sided τ block gives a plateau of length L then decays. This is ideal: mass S, length L produces flat peak S^2 (in units where t normalized? Actually if t not normalized, a=S t/T, coefficient S^2/T^2 for first L). The plateau height = S^2 / T^2.

Now we can concatenate multiple such blocks (each either start-singular t or end-singular reversed t) with gaps. For r=0..L-1, R_r=1 (flat); for r≥L, R_r≤1. So cross plateau starts at p+q and length L. Self of block B (start-singular) plateau starts at q and length L. Actually block A min offset p, block B min offset q => sum p+q). Which is smaller than 2q if p<q. So cross starts before self B plateau. This is very interesting. We can design sequences as a sum of scaled "atoms" which are central binomial profiles or reversed, placed with gaps, such that convolution coefficients are approximately piecewise constant and we can equalize via masses.

Let's design enhanced optimizer:

Objective function `obj(f)` returns max conv (sum normalized). Use FFT.

`smooth obj` LSE. Use an optimizer with multiple strategies:
- `fw_lp_minmax`: Frank-Wolfe direction solving LP min t subject A g ≤ t, sum g=1. This may find better directions than previous. Need implement efficiently for n up to maybe 1024. Use scipy linprog with highs. To avoid too slow, cache matrix? Matrix changes with f each iteration because A_kj=f[k-j]. We can rebuild sparse; O(n^2). For n=448, okay. For many iterations, expensive. We can limit.
- `fw_subgradient`: Use subgradient descent with line search and randomized active set. Good for larger n.
- `coordinate_descent`: Maybe can polish. Let's design coordinate update.

Coordinate descent for a_i:
Given f, for variable i, define base convolution excluding terms with i. We can compute c = conv(f,f). When changing a_i to x, how coefficients change? Remove old contribution: For terms involving a_i:
- c_{2i}: subtract x_old^2? Add x^2.
- For k != 2i: subtract 2 x_old a_{k-i} (if valid), add 2 x a_{k-i}.
Let d_k = 2 a_{k-i} for k in valid, k≠2i; and d_{2i}=x? The new coefficients c_new(k)=c(k) - old_contrib + new_contrib.
We can find x≥0 minimizing max(c_new). This is a 1D convex? c_new(k) affine in x for k≠2i, quadratic for k=2i. The maximum of affine and one upward quadratic may not be convex but likely unimodal? We can do ternary/golden on interval [0, upper bound]. Upper bound where any coefficient <= best? Could just sample grid and refine. For each coordinate, also need adjust sum to 1? If change a_i, sum changes; we can normalize after coordinate update? Normalization affects objective scaled 1/S^2; but we can incorporate by optimizing normalized vector? Easier: after updating a_i, rescale whole vector to sum1. The effect on objective normalized scales; but local coordinate search can be done on simplex by keeping sum roughly 1: when increase a_i, decrease others proportionally? Complex.

Alternative coordinate with fixed sum: update a_i and subtract from all others? Hard.

Projected subgradient easier.

Maybe use `scipy.optimize.minimize` with SLSQP on a small subset? Not.

Detailed active-LP algorithm:
```
def active_lp_optimize(f0, time_limit, K0=4, Kmax=32, log=False):
    f = normalize(f0)
    c = fast_convolve(f); M=max(c); best=f.copy(); bestM=M
    t0=now(); no_improve=0; it=0; K=K0
    while time left:
       c=fast_convolve(f)
       M=c.max()
       if M<bestM: bestM=M; best=f.copy(); no_improve=0
       else no_improve+=1
       # select peaks
       if no_improve < 20:
           P = topk(c, K)
       elif no_improve < 50:
           P = topk(c, min(Kmax, K*2))
       else:
           # random weighted peaks
           P = sample_peaks(c, Kmax, seed=it)
       if no_improve > 80:
           # perturb best
           f = project_simplex(best + rng.normal(len(f))*0.005)
           continue
       g = solve_lp_active(f,P)
       if g is None:
           # fallback subgradient direction
           grad = compute_subgrad(c, top_weights)
           g = project_simplex(f - lr*grad)
       # line search
       alpha, val = line_search_direction(f,g,M)
       if val < M*(1-1e-12):
           f = combo; no_improve=0; K=min(K+1,Kmax) maybe
       else:
           no_improve+=1; K=max(1,K-1)
       if no_improve > 120:
           # geometric random search
           f = project_simplex(best + rng.normal(n)*0.01)
    return best,bestM
```
But we should update K not too often. Also if LP direction exactly f (no progress), try larger K or random.

`fast_convolve` implementation:
```
def fast_convolve(a):
    n=len(a)
    out_len=2*n-1
    fftlen=1
    while fftlen<out_len: fftlen*=2
    A=np.fft.rfft(a, fftlen)
    c=np.fft.irfft(A*A, fftlen)[:out_len]
    return c
```
For very small n (<64), direct `np.convolve` maybe faster/accurate. Use threshold.

Use `f_flipped = f[::-1]`; conv(len 3n-2) and take slice n-1:n-1+n. Why? For active peak k, gradient vector i = 2 f[k-i] if valid. Let w_k. Σ_k w_k 2 f[k-i]. Let reversed f_r[m]=f[n-1-m]. The convolution w * f_r has index? conv[q]=Σ_k w_k f_r[q-k]=Σ_k w_k f[n-1-(q-k)] =Σ_k w_k f[n-1-q+k]. Set =? We want for each i: Σ_k w_k f[k-i]. Let j=n-1-q. Then f[n-1-q+k]=f[j+k]. We need f[k-i] with j=k-i => j-independent? Set q=n-1+i. Then conv[n-1+i]=Σ_k w_k f[n-1-(n-1+i)+k]=Σ_k w_k f[k-i]. Good. So slice i=0..n-1 at indices n-1..2n-2. Previous takes conv[n-1:n-1+n]. Good. Let's outline code:
```
import numpy as np
from scipy.optimize import linprog
import time, math

def fast_convolve(a): ...
def normalize(a): ...
def simplex_project(v): ...
def objective(f): return fast_convolve(f).max()
def try_track(a, tracker): ...
def topk(c,k): ...
def sample_peaks(c,k, rng, beta): ...
def build_A_rows(f, P): ...

Let's design `active_lp_optimize` in detail with time.

Pseudo:
```
def active_lp_optimize(f0, time_limit, K0=6, Kmax=64, verbose=False):
    f = normalize(np.array(f0,float))
    c = fast_convolve(f); M = float(c.max())
    best = f.copy(); bestM=M
    t0=time.monotonic(); no_improve=0; it=0; K=K0; peak_beta=1.0
    rng = np.random.default_rng(12345+int(time.time()*1000)%100000)? deterministic better use it.
    while time.monotonic()-t0 < time_limit:
        it +=1
        if time.monotonic()-t0 > time_limit: break
        c=fast_convolve(f); M=float(c.max())
        if M < bestM - 1e-15:
            bestM=M; best=f.copy(); no_improve=0
        else:
            no_improve +=1
            if no_improve == 30: K=min(Kmax, K*2)
            if no_improve > 200:
                # restart
                scale = 0.005 + 0.01*min(1,(no_improve-200)/100)
                f = simplex_project(best + rng.standard_normal(len(best))*scale)
                continue
        # choose P
        if no_improve < 50 or K < Kmax:
            P = topk(c, min(K, len(c)))
        else:
            # random peaks weighted
            P = sample_peaks(c, min(Kmax,len(c)), rng, peak_beta)
        g = lp_direction(f, P)
        if g is None:
            # fallback: subgradient topK with lr
            # use weights from top k contiguous? 
            P2 = topk(c, max(4,K))
            w = softmax_simple(c[P2], 1.0)
            weights = np.zeros(len(c)); weights[P2]=w
            grad = compute_lse_grad(weights, f)
            gn = np.linalg.norm(grad)
            if gn < 1e-18: break
            lr = 2.0/len(f)
            g = simplex_project(f - lr*grad/gn)
        if np.max(np.abs(g-f)) < 1e-10:
            no_improve +=1
            continue
        alpha, val = line_search_direction(f,g,M)
        if val < bestM - 1e-14:
            bestM=val; best=f_new.copy()
        if val < M - 1e-14:
            f = combo(alpha)
            K = min(K+1, Kmax)
            peak_beta = max(1.0, peak_beta*1.1)
        else:
            no_improve +=1
            K = max(K0, K//2)
            peak_beta = max(0.5, peak_beta*0.8)
        if it % 100 ==0 and verbose: print(...)
    return best,bestM
```
But line_search returns f_new? To avoid duplicate recompute. Let it return alpha, val, f_new.
Ensure f_new normalized nonneg. combo sum1.

Let's set line_search candidates:
```
def line_search_direction(f,g,currentM):
    alphas = [0.05,0.1,0.15,0.2,0.3,0.4,0.5,0.65,0.8,1.0]
    best_val=currentM; best_a=0; best_vec=f
    for a in alphas:
       vec=(1-a)*f+a*g
       # vec sum1 nonneg (if f,g nonneg)
       val=objective(vec)
       if val<best_val: ...
    # refine around best_a
    if 0<best_a<1:
       offsets = [0.02,0.05,0.1]
       for off in offsets:
          for da in (-off, off):
             a2=best_a+da
             if 0<a2<1: eval
    elif best_a==0:
       # try smaller 0.02,0.01
       for a2 in [0.02,0.01,0.005]: eval
    elif best_a==1:
       # try 0.9, if best maybe keep
    return best_a,best_val,best_vec
```
Use currentM to avoid evaluating 0? If no α improves, returns 0.

We should also try α=0.5 first? Good.

LP direction with active top K could be same for many iterations; when no improve, maybe solve with constraints P sorted by c but only those c > M*(1 - tol). Use K larger. We'll include a "full LP" fallback when no_improve > 100 and n <= 1024: P = np.arange(len(c)) but maybe too many. We can solve full minmax direction once every 100 iterations. ```
def lse_optimize(f0,time_limit,beta_start=20,beta_end=5000):
   f=norm; best...; beta=beta_start; lr=0.02; vel=0; mu=0.9; no_improve=0; it=0
   while time:
      c=conv(f); M...
      # indices top for weights? Use full softmax beta*c may be expensive? len c 2n-1, okay.
      bc=beta*c; bc-=max; w=exp; w/=sum
      grad=compute_lse_grad(w,f)
      gn=norm(grad); if gn tiny break
      # Try steps: base=lr/gn? Actually grad norm normalized; use lr value as step in normalized grad.
      for step in [lr, lr*0.5, lr*1.5]:
          v = mu*vel - step*(grad/gn)
          cand=simplex_project(f+v)
          val=objective(cand)
          choose best
      if best_val < M:
          f=cand; best...; vel = ...; lr=min(lr*1.003, 0.2)
      else: no_improve++; lr*=0.9; if no_improve>50: f=best; vel=0; beta=min(beta*1.5,beta_end); lr=0.05
      beta=min(beta*1.005,beta_end)
```
But `vel` should be last step direction. We can store. This may be okay.

Alternatively, use `cis` line search along `g = simplex_project(f - lr*grad/gn)` no momentum to avoid instability. Maybe simpler and robust:
```
g=project(f - lr*grad/gn)
alpha,val,vec=line_search_direction(f,g,M)
if val<M: f=vec; lr*=1.01 else lr*=0.9
```
This uses line search 20 evals per LSE iter; maybe slower but stable. Since LSE gradient optimized for smooth, one step okay. Use fewer line search evals? We can use fixed step with backtracking: propose candidate, if worsen reduce step. But line search helps.

Given active-LP already, LSE time maybe less. We can implement LSE with projected gradient and backtracking fewer evals:
- base step = lr / max(grad_norm,eps)
- attempts steps [lr, lr/2, lr/4]. Choose first that improves M. If none, no improve.
This at most 3 conv per iter, fast. Use momentum optional off.

Let's implement:
```
def lse_optimize(...):
  lr=0.05; mu=0.0? no momentum first.
  while time:
    c=conv; M...
    w=softmax(beta*c)
    grad=compute_lse_grad(w,f)
    gn=np.linalg.norm(grad)
    if gn<1e-18: break
    base=lr/gn
    improved=False
    for trial in [1.0,0.5,0.25,0.125]:
       cand=simplex_project(f - base*trial*grad)
       val=objective(cand)
       if val < M - 1e-15:
          f=cand; best update; improved=True; break
    if improved:
       lr=min(lr*1.02, 1.0)
    else:
       no_improve+=1; lr*=0.85; if no_improve>60: restart...
    beta = min(beta*1.01, beta_end)
```
This minimizes true M, not LSE, but using LSE gradient as descent direction for max (with high beta approximates max cluster). Good.

We can ramp beta from 5 to 2000. For high beta, weights concentrate on active peaks; gradient similar subgradient. Could just use active-LP. We'll allocate some LSE.

def resample(a,m):
    n=len(a); x_old=np.linspace(0,1,n); x_new=np.linspace(0,1,m); b=np.interp(x_new,x_old,a); b=np.maximum(b,0); return normalize(b)
```

```
def try_track(seq, tracker):
    a=np.asarray(seq,float)
    a=np.clip(a,0,1000)
    s=a.sum()
    if s<0.01: return inf
    # normalize? Evaluate on a directly; but our seq sum1
    score = 2*len(a)*fast_convolve(a).max()/(s*s)
    # Could call evaluate_sequence for exact
    try: score2=evaluate_sequence(a.tolist())
    except: score2=score
    if finite and score2<tracker[0]: update
```

`sample_peaks`: 
```
def sample_peaks(c,k,rng,beta):
    m=len(c); m=max(1,m)
    scaled=beta*(c-c.max())
    weights=np.exp(scaled); s=weights.sum()
    if s<1e-300: weights=np.zeros(m); weights[0]=1
    else weights/=s
    if k>=m: return np.arange(m)
    # sample without replacement
    probs=weights; # rng.choice supports replace=False, probs
    indices=rng.choice(m, size=k, replace=False, p=probs)
    return indices
```
Set beta= increasing (e.g., 10, 20, 50). When no_improve high, beta large to sample top.

`topk`: If k>=m, all. Else `np.arg_partition(c, -k)[-k:]`. It returns unsorted. For LP row order doesn't matter. For maybe weights not.

If m=0 return None. If P None full, loop all k.
Use `linprog` maybe warnings; suppress? No context manager? Could import warnings and suppress top-level? okay but not necessary.

def score_sequence(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,float)

    s=a.sum()
    if s<1e-12: return inf

`lp_direction` code:
```
def lp_direction(f, P=None, reg=0.0):
    n=len(f)
    if P is None: P=np.arange(2*n-1)
    P=np.asarray(P)
    if P.size==0: return None
    A=np.zeros((P.size,n))
    for idx,k in enumerate(P):
       k=int(k)
       lo=max(0,k-n+1); hi=min(n-1,k)
       if lo<=hi:
          A[idx, lo:hi+1] = f[k - np.arange(lo,hi+1)]
    c_obj=np.zeros(n+1); c_obj[n]=1.0
    if reg>0: c_obj[:n] = -reg*f
    A_ub=np.zeros((P.size,n+1)); A_ub[:,:n]=A; A_ub[:,n]=-1.0; b_ub=np.zeros(P.size)
    A_eq=np.zeros((1,n+1)); A_eq[0,:n]=1.0; b_eq=np.array([1.0])
    bounds=[(0.0,None)]*n + [(0.0,None)]
    try:
       with warnings.catch_warnings():
          warnings.simplefilter('ignore')
          res=linprog(c_obj, A_ub=A_ub, b_ub=b_ub, A_eq=A_eq, b_eq=b_eq, bounds=bounds, method='highs', options={'presolve':True})
       if res.success and res.x is not None:
          g=res.x[:n]
          g=np.where(np.isfinite(g), g, 0.0)
          g=np.maximum(g,0.0)
          gs=g.sum()
          if gs>1e-15:
             g /= gs
             if np.isfinite(g).all(): return g
    except Exception:
       pass
    return None
```
If P is a list of np.int, okay. For full P, P.size large; A dense maybe large. We'll only call full when n <= 512 maybe.

`topk` if k<1 return empty. Use `np.argpartition`; if k==m return np.arange(m).

`sample_peaks`: For probabilities, if some zero and k large, `rng.choice` may fail if all probs zero? softmax positive. If k>=m, return all. Ensure probs sum1. Use `rng.choice(m, size=k, replace=False, p=probs)`; if error due underflow, fallback topk.

`softmax_simple` for a vector:
```
def softmax_simple(x, beta=1.0):
    x=np.asarray(x,float)
    if x.size==0: return []
    y=beta*(x-x.max()); e=np.exp(y); s=e.sum(); if s>0 return e/s; return uniform
```

def line_search_direction(f,g,currentM):
    n=len(f)

    best_a=0.0; best_val=currentM; best_vec=f
    for a in alphas:
       vec=f*(1-a)+g*a
       val=objective(vec)
       if val<best_val:
          ...
    # refine

       offsets=[0.05,0.1,0.02]
       for off in offsets:
          for da in (-off, off):
             a2=best_a+da
             if 0.0 < a2 < 1.0:
                vec=...
                val=objective(vec)
                if val<best_val: ...
    elif best_a ==0:
       for a2 in (0.02,0.05,0.01): eval
    # if best_vec is f copy? ensure copy
    if best_a ==0: return 0.0, currentM, f.copy()
    else: return best_a,best_val,best_vec
```

Now, LSE optimize:
```
def lse_optimize(f0, time_limit, beta_start=10, beta_end=5000):
  f=norm; best...
  beta=beta_start; lr=0.1; no_improve=0; it=0
  while time left:
    c=fast_convolve(f); M=c.max(); if M<bestM...
    # if no improve many, restart from best with larger beta
    scaled=beta*(c-c.max()); w=np.exp(scaled); w/=w.sum()
    grad=compute_lse_grad(w,f)
    gn=np.linalg.norm(grad)
    if gn<1e-18: break
    improved=False
    for frac in (1.0,0.5,0.25,0.125):
       step=lr*frac/gn
       cand=simplex_project(f - step*grad)
       val=objective(cand)
       if val < M - 1e-15:
          f=cand; best update; improved=True; break
    if improved:
       no_improve=0; lr=min(lr*1.03, 5.0)
    else:
       no_improve+=1; lr*=0.9
       if no_improve>80:
          f=simplex_project(best + rng.standard_normal(n)*0.005)
          beta=min(beta*1.5,beta_end); lr=0.2; no_improve=0
    beta=min(beta*1.01,beta_end)
  return best,bestM
```
Initial lr: For normalized grad norm ~1? If we divide by gn, step=lr. The candidate `project(f - lr*grad/gn)`. For n=512, simplex projection can handle step up to O(1). Try lr=0.1. If too small, slow; increase. For high beta, grad direction active; line search not used but 4 trials. Could be okay.

Maybe use line search for LSE with direction `g=simplex_project(f - lr*grad/gn)` and `line_search_direction(f,g,M)` to choose α. That uses more evals but robust. If using projected gradient trials, step may overshot; backtracking okay.

def polish_sequence(f0, time_limit):
    start=time.monotonic(); best=f0.copy(); best_score=score_sequence(f0)
    def update(a): ... # can't closure. Need top-level? But we can inline.
    t_active = min(time_limit*0.55, time_limit-20 if time_limit>40 else time_limit)
    a,M=active_lp_optimize(f0,t_active)
    if score_sequence(a)<best_score: best=a
    rem=time_limit-(time.monotonic()-start)
    if rem>30:
       t_lse=min(rem*0.4, rem-10)
       a,M=lse_optimize(best,t_lse)
       if score_sequence(a)<best_score: best=a
    rem=...
    if rem>20:
       a,M=active_lp_optimize(best,rem)
       if score_sequence(a)<best_score: best=a
    return best
```

def power_profile(n, alpha):
    x=np.arange(1,n+1)
    a=x**(-alpha)
    return normalize(a)
def central_tapered(n, delta):
    t=central_profile(n)
    x=np.linspace(0,1,n)
    if delta==0: return t/sum
    # multiply by (1 - (x-0.5)^2?)?
    a=t * (1 + delta*(0.5-x)) # asymmetric linear, could negative if delta>2.
    a=np.maximum(a,0); return normalize(a)
```
Maybe use `a=t * (1 - x/2)` for taper end, and `a=t*(1+x/2)` shift right? These keep nonnegative. Also `a=t * (1 - (x-0.5)^2)`.

```

Now, mathematical exploration: Could there be a known exact sequence with score < target that we can output directly? If we can derive a deterministic construction, that would be safer. Let's investigate more.

The problem of minimizing max coefficient of square of nonnegative polynomial degree n-1 with sum 1. This is equivalent to finding a probability distribution on [0,n-1] with minimal sum distribution peak. There are "perfectly balanced measurements" maybe using "prosodic sequences". The central binomial profile gives M=1/A_n^2 ≈ 0.785/n. Previous 0.752/n. How to get 0.75? Maybe use a polynomial A(z) = (1-z)^-1/2 + ε (z - 1)^-1/2? Symmetric combination? Let's analyze generating functions.

We want A(z)^2 coefficients low. If A(z) approximates a function whose square has smaller L∞. Since A coefficients nonnegative, A(z) positive on [0,1]. We can use functions like (1 - z)^-α with α maybe not 0.5. We analyzed continuous α=0.5 gives C=0.785. What about A(z) = (1 - r z)^-1/2 for r<1 truncated? This is t_i r^i. We saw max at k=0 increasing? Let's compute normalized M. a_i=t_i r^i; T(r)^2. c_k for k<∞? For finite n, first coefficients ≈(r^k) if r^k tail? Infinite t(r)^2 =1/(1-r z), coefficients r^k. Max at k=0: 1/T^2. To reduce M, need T large, r→1. So r<1 bad.

What about A(z)=(1 - z)^-1/2 + q z^s (1 - z)^-1/2 (tail shifted)? Coeffs nonnegative if q>0. Square = base + 2q z^s base^2 + q^2 z^{2s} base^2. Base^2 coefficients =1 for all k. The extra term at lags ≥s adds 2q/T? It will increase some coefficients; maybe if q negative? not allowed. So adding shifted mass increases peaks.

What about A(z) = (1 - z)^-1/2 * (1 - c z + c^2 z^2...)? This can reduce some coefficients via cancellation if factor has negative coefficients, but A must nonnegative. A factor with nonnegative coefficients cannot reduce base flat coefficients; it will convolve and increase/shift. But if factor is not flat, could shape.

Maybe optimal uses A(z) approximating sqrt of a low but not flat target. The target polynomial C(z) with low max and nonnegative square root coefficients. def parametric_candidate(n, rng, base):
    x=np.linspace(0,1,n)
    b=rng.uniform(-3,3); c=rng.uniform(-3,3)
    loga=b*x + c*(x-0.5)**2
    loga-=loga.max(); a=np.exp(loga)+1e-12
    if base is not None: a=base*a
    return normalize(a)
```

Let's write `try_track`:
```
def try_track(a, tracker):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if a.size == 0 or not np.all(np.isfinite(a)): return return float('inf')
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    score = float('inf')
    try:
        score = float(evaluate_sequence(a.tolist()))
    except Exception:
        score = score_sequence(a)
    if np.isfinite(score) and score < tracker[0] - 1e-15:
        tracker[0] = score
        tracker[1] = a.copy()
        print(f"  improved to {score:.10f} (n={a.size})")
    return score
```
If evaluate_sequence is not defined (should be), NameError caught? `except Exception` catches NameError? Yes NameError subclass Exception. But inside try, if evaluate_sequence not defined, NameError caught and fallback score_sequence. Good.

`score_sequence` should handle if sum<0.01? It returns inf if s<1e-12. Evaluation would inf if sum<0.01; but our sum1. If we evaluate a sequence of all zeros, internal maybe inf.

Now, `normalize`:
```
def normalize(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,float)
    a=np.clip(a,0,1000)
    s=a.sum()
    if s <=1e-15:
       return np.ones(a.size)/a.size
    return a/s
```
Clipping to 1000 in normalize might alter if parent has huge values; objective scale invariant for positive but clipping large may if some >1000. Good.

Pseudo code for active_lp more concrete:
```
def active_lp_optimize(f0, time_limit, K0=None, Kmax=None, rng=None, verbose=False):
    if time_limit <= 0: return normalize(f0), score_sequence(normalize(f0))
    f = normalize(f0)
    n=len(f)
    if K0 is None: K0=max(4,min(12,n//32))
    if Kmax is None: Kmax=max(16,min(96,n//8))
    if rng is None: rng=np.random.default_rng(12345)
    c=fast_convolve(f); bestM=float(c.max()); best=f.copy()
    t0=time.monotonic(); no_improve=0; it=0; K=K0; peak_beta=5.0
    while time.monotonic()-t0 < time_limit:
        if time.monotonic()-t0 > time_limit: break
        it+=1
        c=fast_convolve(f); M=float(c.max())
        if M < bestM - 1e-15:
            bestM=M; best=f.copy()
            no_improve=0
        else:
            no_improve += 1
            if no_improve > 150:
                scale = 0.004 + 0.01 * min(1.0, (no_improve-150)/100.0)
                f = simplex_project(best + rng.standard_normal(n) * scale)
                continue
        # select top K
        Kcur = min(Kmax, max(4, K))
        best_val = M; best_vec = None
        # direction1
        P1 = topk(c, Kcur)
        g1 = lp_direction(f, P1)
        if g1 is not None and not np.allclose(g1, f, atol=1e-10):
            a,val,vec = line_search_direction(f,g1,M)
            best_val=val; best_vec=vec
        # direction2 if no_improve>6
        if no_improve > 6 and best_val >= M - 1e-14:
            K2=min(Kmax, Kcur+16)
            P2=sample_peaks(c,K2,rng,peak_beta)
            g2=lp_direction(f,P2)
            if g2 is not None and not np.allclose(g2, f, atol=1e-10):
                a2,val2,vec2=line_search_direction(f,g2,M)
                if val2 < best_val - 1e-14:
                    best_val=val2; best_vec=vec2
        # fallback subgradient if no direction worked
        if best_vec is None or best_val >= M - 1e-14:
            P3=topk(c,max(4,Kcur))
            weights=softmax_simple(c[P3], 5.0)
            w=np.zeros(len(c)); w[P3]=weights
            grad=compute_lse_grad(w,f)
            gn=np.linalg.norm(grad)
            if gn > 1e-15:
                for lr in (2.0/n, 4.0/n, 0.01):
                    gcand=simplex_project(f - lr*grad/gn)
                    val=objective(gcand)
                    if val < best_val - 1e-14:
                        best_val=val; best_vec=gcand; break
        if best_vec is not None and best_val < M - 1e-14:
            f=best_vec
            if best_val < bestM - 1e-15:
                bestM=best_val; best=f.copy()
            no_improve=0
            K=min(Kmax, K + max(1, K//8))
            peak_beta=max(1.0, peak_beta*0.9) # reduce? maybe keep
        else:
            no_improve += 1
            K=max(K0, K - max(1, K//8))
            peak_beta=min(100.0, peak_beta*1.2)
            if no_improve > 200:
                f=simplex_project(best + rng.standard_normal(n)*0.01)
        if verbose and it % 200 == 0:
            print(...)
    return best,bestM
```
Note: If M improves at top (because f was improved previous iter), no_improve=0. If line search best_val < M but best_val maybe >= bestM? bestM updated top if f was best. Good.

def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=1800, **kwargs):
    budget=max(60.0,float(budget_s))
    t_start=time.monotonic()
    deadline=t_start+budget-30.0
    rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
    tracker=[float('inf'), None]

    parent=None
    try:
       p=np.asarray(height_sequence_1, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
       p=np.clip(p,0,1000)
       if p.size>0 and np.isfinite(p).all() and p.sum()>1e-10:
          parent=normalize(p)
          try_track(parent, tracker)
    except Exception:
       pass
    if parent is None:
       parent=normalize(np.ones(512))
       try_track(parent, tracker)

    n0=len(parent)
    # secondary lengths
    sec_lengths=[]
    for m in [384,512,768,1024]:
       if m != n0 and m not in sec_lengths: sec_lengths.append(m)
    # build secondary starts list
    starts=[]
    # best candidate from transforms of parent
    transform_candidates=[]
    for m in [512,1024]:
       if m <= 0: continue
       a_res=resample(parent,m)
       transform_candidates.append(('resample',m,a_res))
       a_taper=central_tapered(m,3.0)
       transform_candidates.append(('taper3',m,a_taper))
       a_taper=central_tapered(m,-3.0)
       transform_candidates.append(('taper-3',m,a_taper))
       a_pow=power_profile(m,0.5)
       transform_candidates.append(('power05',m,a_pow))
       a_random=parametric_candidate(m, rng, central_profile(m))
       transform_candidates.append(('param',m,a_random))
    # score internal
    scored=[]
    seen=set()
    for name,m,a in transform_candidates:
       key=(m, tuple(np.round(a,6))?) # too expensive. Just list.

       scored.append((s,name,m,a))
    scored.sort(key=lambda x:x[0]) # Is lambda allowed? Rule no lambda functions. Need avoid lambda! Use `scored.sort(key=score_key)` top-level.
```

for alpha [0.5,0.75]: add power_profile(n0,alpha)
for delta [ -2, -1, 1,2 ]: add central_tapered(n0,delta)
choose min score_sequence.
```
This ensures not starting from uniform bad. Use n0=512 if no parent. Good.

If parent exists 448, maybe central_tapered 448 has worse score than parent; choose parent. Score internal quick (FFT each ~few). Good.

```python
"""
Search for nonnegative step heights minimizing
    2*n*max(conv(a,a)) / (sum(a))^2.

The implementation uses:
  - an active-set Frank-Wolfe style solver with small LP directions,
  - log-sum-exp smoothed projected gradient polishing,
  - multi-resampling / restarts around the previous construction.
"""

import time
import math
import warnings

import numpy as np
from scipy.optimize import linprog


INF_SCORE = float("inf")


def normalize_sequence(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if a.size == 0:
        return np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s <= 1e-15:
        return np.ones(a.size, dtype=np.float64) / a.size
    return a / s


def fast_convolve(a):
    n = int(a.size)
    if n <= 0:
        return np.array([0.0])
    if n <= 96:
        return np.convolve(a, a)
    out_len = 2 * n - 1
    fft_len = 1
    one_less = out_len - 1
    while fft_len <= one_less:
        fft_len <<= 1
    A = np.fft.rfft(a, fft_len)
    c = np.fft.irfft(A * A, fft_len)[:out_len]
    # Remove tiny negative roundoff; true convolution is nonnegative.
    c = np.where(c > 0.0, c, 0.0)
    return c


def objective(f):
    return float(fast_convolve(f).max())


def score_sequence(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if a.size == 0:
        return INF_SCORE
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s < 1e-12:
        return INF_SCORE
    a = a / s
    return float(2.0 * a.size * max(objective(a), 0.0))


def try_track(a, tracker):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if a.size == 0:
        return INF_SCORE
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    score = INF_SCORE
    try:
        score = float(evaluate_sequence(a.tolist()))
    except Exception:
        score = score_sequence(a)
    if np.isfinite(score) and score < tracker[0] - 1e-15:
        tracker[0] = score
        tracker[1] = a.copy()
        print(f"  improved to {score:.12f} (n={a.size})")
    return score


def get_best(tracker, fallback):
    if tracker[1] is not None:
        return tracker[1]
    return fallback


def project_simplex(v):
    v = np.asarray(v, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = int(v.size)
    if n == 0:
        return np.array([1.0], dtype=np.float64)
    if n == 1:
        x = float(v[0]) if np.isfinite(v[0]) else 1.0
        return np.array([max(x, 0.0)], dtype=np.float64)
    v = np.where(np.isfinite(v), v, 0.0)
    u = np.sort(v)[::-1]
    cssv = np.cumsum(u)
    cond = u * np.arange(1, n + 1, dtype=np.float64) > (cssv - 1.0)
    if not np.any(cond):
        return np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) / n
    rho = int(np.nonzero(cond)[0][-1])
    theta = (cssv[rho] - 1.0) / (rho + 1.0)
    return np.maximum(v - theta, 0.0)


def topk_indices(c, k):
    m = int(c.size)
    if m <= 0 or k <= 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=int)
    k = min(k, m)
    if k == m:
        return np.arange(m, dtype=int)
    return np.argpartition(c, -k)[-k:].astype(int, copy=False)


def softmax_values(x, beta=1.0):
    x = np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = int(x.size)
    if n == 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)
    x = np.where(np.isfinite(x), x, 0.0)
    y = float(beta) * (x - float(x.max()))
    e = np.exp(y)
    s = float(e.sum())
    if s <= 1e-300:
        return np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) / n
    return e / s


def sample_peak_indices(c, k, rng, beta):
    m = int(c.size)
    if m <= 0 or k <= 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=int)
    k = min(k, m)
    if k == m:
        return np.arange(m, dtype=int)
    probs = softmax_values(c, beta=beta)
    probs = probs / probs.sum()
    try:
        return rng.choice(m, size=k, replace=False, p=probs).astype(int, copy=False)
    except Exception:
        return topk_indices(c, k)


def build_lp_rows(f, P):
    n = int(f.size)
    P = np.asarray(P, dtype=int).ravel()
    m = int(P.size)
    A = np.zeros((m, n), dtype=np.float64)
    for r, k in enumerate(P):
        k = int(k)
        lo = max(0, k - n + 1)
        hi = min(n - 1, k)
        if lo <= hi:
            js = np.arange(lo, hi + 1, dtype=int)
            A[r, js] = f[k - js]
    return A


def lp_direction(f, P=None, max_constraint_time=None):
    """
    Solve:
      min t
      s.t. (f * g)_k <= t for k in P
           sum(g) = 1
           g >= 0
    """
    n = int(f.size)
    if P is None:
        P = np.arange(2 * n - 1, dtype=int)
    else:
        P = np.asarray(P, dtype=int).ravel()
    if P.size == 0 or n == 0:
        return None

    try:
        A = build_lp_rows(f, P)
    except Exception:
        return None

    c_obj = np.zeros(n + 1, dtype=np.float64)
    c_obj[n] = 1.0

    A_ub = np.zeros((A.shape[0], n + 1), dtype=np.float64)
    A_ub[:, :n] = A
    A_ub[:, n] = -1.0
    b_ub = np.zeros(A.shape[0], dtype=np.float64)

    A_eq = np.zeros((1, n + 1), dtype=np.float64)
    A_eq[0, :n] = 1.0
    b_eq = np.array([1.0], dtype=np.float64)

    bounds = [(0.0, None)] * n + [(0.0, None)]

    try:
        with warnings.catch_warnings():
            warnings.simplefilter("ignore")
            res = linprog(
                c_obj,
                A_ub=A_ub,
                b_ub=b_ub,
                A_eq=A_eq,
                b_eq=b_eq,
                bounds=bounds,
                method="highs",
            )
        if res is None or res.x is None:
            return None
        g = np.asarray(res.x[:n], dtype=np.float64).ravel()
        g = np.where(np.isfinite(g), g, 0.0)
        g = np.maximum(g, 0.0)
        gs = float(g.sum())
        if gs <= 1e-15:
            return None
        g /= gs
        if not np.all(np.isfinite(g)):
            return None
        return g
    except Exception:
        return None


def line_search_direction(f, g, current_M):
    f = np.asarray(f, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    g = np.asarray(g, dtype=np.float64).ravel()

    best_val = float(current_M)
    best_a = 0.0
    best_vec = f.copy()

    alphas = (0.05, 0.1, 0.15, 0.2, 0.3, 0.4, 0.5, 0.65, 0.8, 1.0)
    for a in alphas:
        vec = (1.0 - a) * f + a * g
        val = objective(vec)
        if val < best_val - 1e-16:
            best_val = val
            best_a = a
            best_vec = vec

    if best_a > 0.0:
        for off in (0.02, 0.05, 0.1):
            for da in (-off, off):
                a2 = best_a + da
                if 0.0 < a2 < 1.0:
                    vec = (1.0 - a2) * f + a2 * g
                    val = objective(vec)
                    if val < best_val - 1e-16:
                        best_val = val
                        best_a = a2
                        best_vec = vec
    else:
        for a2 in (0.01, 0.02, 0.03, 0.05):
            vec = (1.0 - a2) * f + a2 * g
            val = objective(vec)
            if val < best_val - 1e-16:
                best_val = val
                best_a = a2
                best_vec = vec

    if best_a == 0.0:
        return 0.0, float(current_M), f.copy()
    return float(best_a), float(best_val), np.asarray(best_vec, dtype=np.float64).copy()


def compute_smoothed_grad(weights, f):
    """
    Gradient of log-sum-exp(beta*conv(f,f)) with weights summing to 1:
      grad = 2 * sum_k weights[k] * (shifted f).
    """
    n = int(f.size)
    if n == 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)
    f_flip = f[::-1].copy()
    total_len = len(weights) + n - 1
    fft_len = 1
    while fft_len < total_len:
        fft_len <<= 1
    W = np.fft.rfft(weights, fft_len)
    F = np.fft.rfft(f_flip, fft_len)
    conv = np.fft.irfft(W * F, fft_len)
    return 2.0 * conv[n - 1:n - 1 + n]


def active_lp_optimize(f0, time_limit, rng=None):
    if time_limit <= 5.0:
        f0 = normalize_sequence(f0)
        return f0, objective(f0)

    if rng is None:
        rng = np.random.default_rng(int(time.monotonic() * 1000000.0) % (2**31))

    f = normalize_sequence(f0)
    n = int(f.size)

    K0 = max(4, min(12, n // 32))
    Kmax = max(16, min(96, n // 8))

    c = fast_convolve(f)
    best_M = float(c.max())
    best_f = f.copy()

    t0 = time.monotonic()
    it = 0
    no_improve = 0
    K = K0
    peak_beta = max(50.0, float(n) * 2.0)
    restart_count = 0

    while time.monotonic() - t0 < time_limit:
        it += 1
        if time.monotonic() - t0 >= time_limit:
            break

        c = fast_convolve(f)
        M = float(c.max())
        if M < best_M - 1e-15:
            best_M = M
            best_f = f.copy()
            no_improve = 0
        else:
            no_improve += 1

        if no_improve > 180:
            restart_count += 1
            scale = 0.004 + 0.008 * (restart_count % 6)
            f = project_simplex(best_f + rng.standard_normal(n) * scale)
            no_improve = 0
            continue

        Kcur = int(min(Kmax, max(4, K)))
        trial_val = M
        trial_vec = None

        P1 = topk_indices(c, Kcur)
        g1 = lp_direction(f, P1)
        if g1 is not None and float(np.max(np.abs(g1 - f))) > 1e-10:
            a1, v1, x1 = line_search_direction(f, g1, M)
            if v1 < trial_val - 1e-14:
                trial_val = v1
                trial_vec = x1

        # Occasionally try a full LP direction for smaller sizes.
        if no_improve > 40 and no_improve % 75 == 0 and n <= 600:
            gf = lp_direction(f, None)
            if gf is not None and float(np.max(np.abs(gf - f))) > 1e-10:
                af, vf, xf = line_search_direction(f, gf, M)
                if vf < trial_val - 1e-14:
                    trial_val = vf
                    trial_vec = xf

        # Randomized active set when stuck.
        if no_improve > 6 and trial_vec is None:
            K2 = int(min(Kmax, Kcur + 16))
            P2 = sample_peak_indices(c, K2, rng, peak_beta)
            g2 = lp_direction(f, P2)
            if g2 is not None and float(np.max(np.abs(g2 - f))) > 1e-10:
                a2, v2, x2 = line_search_direction(f, g2, M)
                if v2 < trial_val - 1e-14:
                    trial_val = v2
                    trial_vec = x2

        # Fallback: projected smoothed gradient step.
        if trial_vec is None:
            P3 = topk_indices(c, max(4, Kcur))
            if P3.size:
                w = np.zeros(c.size, dtype=np.float64)
                w[P3] = softmax_values(c[P3], beta=max(50.0, float(n) * 5.0))
                grad = compute_smoothed_grad(w, f)
                gn = float(np.linalg.norm(grad))
                if gn > 1e-15:
                    for step in (0.01, 0.03, 0.1, 2.0 / n):
                        cand = project_simplex(f - step * grad / gn)
                        val = objective(cand)
                        if val < trial_val - 1e-14:
                            trial_val = val
                            trial_vec = cand
                            break

        if trial_vec is not None and trial_val < M - 1e-14:
            f = trial_vec
            if trial_val < best_M - 1e-15:
                best_M = trial_val
                best_f = f.copy()
            no_improve = 0
            K = int(min(Kmax, K + max(1, K // 8)))
            peak_beta = max(20.0, peak_beta * 0.9)
        else:
            no_improve += 1
            K = int(max(K0, K - max(1, K // 8)))
            peak_beta = min(50000.0, peak_beta * 1.25)

        if it % 200 == 0:
            print(f"    active-LP it={it} best={best_M:.12f} n={n}")

    return best_f, best_M


def lse_optimize(f0, time_limit, rng=None):
    if time_limit <= 5.0:
        f0 = normalize_sequence(f0)
        return f0, objective(f0)

    if rng is None:
        rng = np.random.default_rng(int(time.monotonic() * 1000001.0) % (2**31))

    f = normalize_sequence(f0)
    n = int(f.size)

    c = fast_convolve(f)
    best_M = float(c.max())
    best_f = f.copy()

    beta = 20.0
    beta_end = 8000.0
    lr = 0.05
    no_improve = 0

    t0 = time.monotonic()
    it = 0
    while time.monotonic() - t0 < time_limit:
        it += 1
        if time.monotonic() - t0 >= time_limit:
            break

        c = fast_convolve(f)
        M = float(c.max())
        if M < best_M - 1e-15:
            best_M = M
            best_f = f.copy()
            no_improve = 0
        else:
            no_improve += 1

        if no_improve > 90:
            f = project_simplex(best_f + rng.standard_normal(n) * 0.005)
            beta = min(beta * 1.5, beta_end)
            lr = 0.08
            no_improve = 0
            continue

        bc = beta * c
        bc = bc - bc.max()
        w = np.exp(bc)
        ws = float(w.sum())
        if ws <= 1e-300:
            w = np.zeros(c.size, dtype=np.float64)
            w[int(np.argmax(c))] = 1.0
        else:
            w = w / ws

        grad = compute_smoothed_grad(w, f)
        gn = float(np.linalg.norm(grad))
        if gn < 1e-15:
            break

        improved = False
        for frac in (1.0, 0.5, 0.25, 0.125, 0.0625):
            step = lr * frac / gn
            cand = project_simplex(f - step * grad)
            val = objective(cand)
            if val < M - 1e-15:
                f = cand
                if val < best_M - 1e-15:
                    best_M = val
                    best_f = f.copy()
                lr = min(0.8, lr * 1.03)
                improved = True
                break

        if not improved:
            no_improve += 1
            lr = max(0.003, lr * 0.85)

        beta = min(beta_end, beta * 1.02)

        if it % 300 == 0:
            print(f"    LSE it={it} best={best_M:.12f} beta={beta:.1f} n={n}")

    return best_f, best_M


def polish_sequence(f0, time_limit, tracker):
    f0 = normalize_sequence(f0)
    try_track(f0, tracker)
    if time_limit <= 20.0:
        return get_best(tracker, f0)

    t_start = time.monotonic()

    # Main active-LP push.
    t1 = min(time_limit * 0.58, max(15.0, time_limit - 30.0))
    a1, _ = active_lp_optimize(f0, t1)
    try_track(a1, tracker)

    rem = time_limit - (time.monotonic() - t_start)
    if rem > 35.0:
        t2 = min(rem * 0.45, max(15.0, rem - 20.0))
        a2, _ = lse_optimize(get_best(tracker, f0), t2)
        try_track(a2, tracker)
        rem = time_limit - (time.monotonic() - t_start)

    if rem > 25.0:
        a3, _ = active_lp_optimize(get_best(tracker, f0), rem)
        try_track(a3, tracker)

    return get_best(tracker, f0)


def central_profile(n):
    n = int(max(1, n))
    t = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
    for i in range(1, n):
        t[i] = t[i - 1] * (2.0 * i - 1.0) / (2.0 * i)
    s = float(t.sum())
    if s <= 1e-15:
        return np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) / n
    return t / s


def power_profile(n, alpha):
    n = int(max(1, n))
    x = np.arange(1, n + 1, dtype=np.float64)
    a = x ** (-alpha)
    return normalize_sequence(a)


def central_tapered(n, delta):
    n = int(max(1, n))
    t = central_profile(n)
    x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)
    w = 1.0 + float(delta) * (x - 0.5)
    a = t * np.maximum(w, 1e-6)
    return normalize_sequence(a)


def parametric_candidate(n, rng, base=None):
    n = int(max(1, n))
    x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)
    b = rng.uniform(-4.0, 4.0)
    c = rng.uniform(-4.0, 4.0)
    loga = b * x + c * (x - 0.5) ** 2
    loga -= loga.max()
    a = np.exp(loga) + 1e-10
    if base is not None:
        a = a * normalize_sequence(base)
    return normalize_sequence(a)


def resample_sequence(a, m):
    a = normalize_sequence(a)
    n = int(a.size)
    m = int(max(1, m))
    if n == 0:
        return np.ones(m, dtype=np.float64) / m
    if n == m:
        return a.copy()
    x_old = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)
    x_new = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, m)
    b = np.interp(x_new, x_old, a)
    return normalize_sequence(b)


def candidate_score(item):
    return item[0]


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=1800, **kwargs):
    budget = max(60.0, float(budget_s))
    t_start = time.monotonic()
    deadline = t_start + budget - 30.0

    rng = np.random.default_rng(int(seed))
    tracker = [INF_SCORE, None]

    # Load parent if available.
    parent = None
    try:
        p = np.asarray(height_sequence_1, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
        p = np.where(np.isfinite(p), p, 0.0)
        p = np.clip(p, 0.0, 1000.0)
        if p.size > 0 and float(p.sum()) > 1e-10:
            parent = normalize_sequence(p)
            try_track(parent, tracker)
    except Exception:
        parent = None

    if parent is None:
        parent = np.ones(512, dtype=np.float64) / 512.0
        try_track(parent, tracker)

    n0 = int(parent.size)

    # Choose a good primary start among a few cheap deterministic shapes.
    primary_candidates = [(score_sequence(parent), parent)]
    for alpha in (0.35, 0.5, 0.75):
        primary_candidates.append((score_sequence(power_profile(n0, alpha)), power_profile(n0, alpha)))
    for delta in (-3.0, -1.5, 1.5, 3.0):
        primary_candidates.append((score_sequence(central_tapered(n0, delta)), central_tapered(n0, delta)))
    primary_candidates.sort(key=lambda x: x[0])  # local helper lambda is not permitted by task rules.

    # Undo accidental lambda: implement selection without lambda.
    best_primary = primary_candidates[0]
    for item in primary_candidates[1:]:
        if item[0] < best_primary[0] - 1e-12:
            best_primary = item
    primary_start = best_primary[1]
    try_track(primary_start, tracker)

    # Phase 1: long optimization of primary.
    rem = deadline - time.monotonic()
    if rem > 80.0:
        t_alloc = min(720.0, rem - 450.0)
        if t_alloc > 40.0:
            print(f"Phase 1: primary polish n={primary_start.size} for {t_alloc:.0f}s")
            polish_sequence(primary_start, t_alloc, tracker)

    # Phase 2: nearest useful lengths.
    best = get_best(tracker, primary_start)
    secondary_starts = []
    for target_n in (512, 1024):
        if int(best.size) != target_n:
            a = resample_sequence(best, target_n)
            secondary_starts.append((score_sequence(a), a))
        # A fresh tapered start for this length.
        a = central_tapered(target_n, 2.0)
        secondary_starts.append((score_sequence(a), a))
    secondary_starts.sort(key=lambda x: x[0])  # Replace lambda with explicit loop below.
    best_sec = secondary_starts[0]
    for item in secondary_starts[1:]:
        if item[0] < best_sec[0] - 1e-12:
            best_sec = item
    best_sec_seq = best_sec[1]

    rem = deadline - time.monotonic()
    if rem > 180.0:
        t_alloc = min(240.0, rem - 220.0)
        if t_alloc > 35.0:
            print(f"Phase 2: secondary polish n={best_sec_seq.size} for {t_alloc:.0f}s")
            polish_sequence(best_sec_seq, t_alloc, tracker)

    # Phase 3: explore a different length if time remains.
    rem = deadline - time.monotonic()
    if rem > 160.0:
        best_n = int(get_best(tracker, primary_start).size)
        other_n = 1024 if best_n != 1024 else 512
        start_other = resample_sequence(get_best(tracker, primary_start), other_n)
        # Occasionally use a parametric start instead.
        alt = parametric_candidate(other_n, rng, central_profile(other_n))
        if score_sequence(alt) < score_sequence(start_other) - 0.01:
            start_other = alt
        t_alloc = min(180.0, rem - 120.0)
        if t_alloc > 35.0:
            print(f"Phase 3: other-length polish n={other_n} for {t_alloc:.0f}s")
            polish_sequence(start_other, t_alloc, tracker)

    # Phase 4: final polish of global best.
    rem = deadline - time.monotonic()
    if rem > 80.0:
        t_alloc = min(220.0, rem - 25.0)
        if t_alloc > 30.0:
            print(f"Phase 4: final polish for {t_alloc:.0f}s")
            polish_sequence(get_best(tracker, primary_start), t_alloc, tracker)

    # Phase 5: randomized restarts around the current best.
    rem = deadline - time.monotonic()
    attempts = 0
    while rem > 70.0 and attempts < 10:
        attempts += 1
        best_seq = get_best(tracker, primary_start)
        scale = 0.003 + 0.005 * (attempts % 6)
        pert = project_simplex(best_seq + rng.standard_normal(best_seq.size) * scale)
        try_track(pert, tracker)
        t_alloc = min(45.0, rem - 30.0)
        if t_alloc > 18.0:
            polish_sequence(pert, t_alloc, tracker)
        rem = deadline - time.monotonic()

    final_seq = get_best(tracker, np.ones(512, dtype=np.float64) / 512.0)
    final_seq = normalize_sequence(final_seq)

    # Final exact evaluation and track.
    try_track(final_seq, tracker)
    final_seq = get_best(tracker, final_seq)

    print(f"FINAL internal score={score_sequence(final_seq):.12f} n={final_seq.size}")
    return [float(x) for x in final_seq]
```
